<result>Based on my review of the orchestrator-pattern documentation, here is the answer:

## id: X9

## question: "According to library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html (and the other guides in that folder where piecing-it-together points to them), what are the named artifacts (files) the orchestrator pattern produces across a launch — spec, tests map, plan, run log, run report, kickoff, endings, review — what does each contain at a high level, and in what order are they produced? Also report: does the source describe any CLI or command surface as part of the pattern, or does it describe the artifacts as plain files an agent reads and writes directly? Quote the passages that settle this."

## stage: intent

## answer:

The orchestrator pattern produces eight named artifacts in this sequence: (1) **spec.md** — the frozen behaviour specification in nine parts, committed at a commit hash that becomes the spec's identity; (2) **test mapping and acceptance script** — named checks mapped to spec B/E numbers, baseline runs confirming tests fail correctly; (3) **kickoff.md** — the run conduct document specifying agents, gates, budgets, and model tiers; (4) **plan.md** — the orchestrator's units of work, interfaces, ordering, and agent assignments; (5) **checks/** — individual check outputs (command, exit code, output) captured verbatim through a wrapper; (6) **review.md** — reviewer findings with severity and state; (7) **report.md** — assembled from events, checks, plan and findings, reconciling planned against landed; (8) **RUNLOG.md** — human-written diagnosis entries added newest-first at stage 10. The pattern describes **plain files that agents read and write directly**, not a CLI surface. The kickoff calls agents to read files ("the orchestrator reads the spec, the test mapping, the constitution and prior reports" [piecing-it-together.html:257]), workers receive "interface files committed" and "mapped checks" as files [piecing-it-together.html:287], and artifacts move by "files and schema-validated returns; workers do not talk to each other" [piecing-it-together.html:315]. Hooks and scripts may run commands, but agents interface via files: "Render the prompt deterministically, with a small skill or script over the two JSON files, rather than having the orchestrator compose it freehand" [core-stages-4-7.html:299].

## confidence: certain

## pointers:
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:200-216 — Stage 1 outputs spec.md at commit
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:218-234 — Stage 2 outputs test mapping and acceptance script
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:236-250 — Stage 3 outputs kickoff.md
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:252-266 — Stage 4 outputs plan.md
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:328-341 — Stage 7 outputs checks/
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:343-356 — Stage 8 outputs review.md and findings
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:358-370 — Stage 9 outputs report.md
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:385-397 — Stage 10 outputs merged commit and RUNLOG.md entry
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:457-481 — Folder structure showing all artifacts as files
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:315 — "files and schema-validated returns; workers do not talk to each other"
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/piecing-it-together.html:257 — "the orchestrator reads the spec, the test mapping, the constitution and prior reports"
- library/source/orchestrator-pattern/core-stages-4-7.html:299 — JSON files rendered deterministically with skill or script, not freehand

cat > "$SP/render-interview.mjs" <<'EOF'
// Usage: node render-interview.mjs <interview-dir> <title> <out.html>
// Renders every bundle with state "open" under <interview-dir>/bundles/ into one page
// in the filebox layout (dev/workspace/filebox/spec-interviewer-interview.html).
import fs from 'node:fs'; import path from 'node:path';
const [dir, title, out] = process.argv.slice(2);
const esc = s => String(s ?? '').replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;').replace(/>/g,'&gt;').replace(/"/g,'&quot;');
const md = s => esc(s).replace(/`([^`]+)`/g, '<code>$1</code>');
const bundles = fs.readdirSync(path.join(dir,'bundles')).filter(f=>f.endsWith('.json')).sort()
  .map(f=>JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir,'bundles',f),'utf8'))).filter(b=>b.state==='open');
const problems = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir,'problems.json'),'utf8'));
const kindOf = m => (problems.find(p=>p.id===m)||{}).kind || '';
let body = '';
for (const b of bundles) {
  body += `\n<div class="bundle" data-marker="${esc(b.marker)}">\n<h2>bundle <code>${esc(b.marker)}</code> · ${esc(kindOf(b.marker))} · ${esc(b.domain)}</h2>\n<p class="problem">${md(b.problem)}</p>\n`;
  for (const q of b.questions) {
    body += `<div class="q" data-id="${esc(q.id)}">\n  <h3><span>${esc(q.id)}</span>${md(q.text)}</h3>\n  <p class="why">${md(q.why)}</p>\n`;
    for (const o of q.options||[]) body += `  <label class="opt"><input type="radio" name="${esc(q.id)}" value="${esc(o.value)}"> ${md(o.value)}${o.recommended?' <span class="rec">★ recommended</span>':''}</label>\n`;
    if (q.allow_comment !== false) body += `  <textarea name="${esc(q.id)}_c" placeholder="Comment (or write your own answer here and leave the options blank)"></textarea>\n`;
    body += `</div>\n`;
  }
  if (b.allow_bundle_comment !== false) body += `<textarea name="${esc(b.marker)}_bc" placeholder="Bundle comment"></textarea>\n`;
  body += `</div>\n`;
}
const html = `<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<title>${esc(title)}</title>
<style>
  :root { --bg:#0f1115; --panel:#171a21; --line:#2a2f3a; --fg:#e6e6e6; --dim:#9aa3b2; --acc:#7cc4ff; --rec:#3ddc97; }
  body { margin:0; font:15px/1.5 -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "Segoe UI", sans-serif; background:var(--bg); color:var(--fg); }
  header { position:sticky; top:0; background:var(--bg); border-bottom:1px solid var(--line); padding:12px 24px; display:flex; gap:16px; align-items:center; z-index:2; }
  header h1 { font-size:16px; margin:0; flex:1; }
  button { background:var(--acc); color:#000; border:0; border-radius:6px; padding:8px 14px; font-weight:600; cursor:pointer; }
  button.ghost { background:transparent; color:var(--acc); border:1px solid var(--acc); }
  main { max-width:960px; margin:0 auto; padding:24px; }
  .lead { color:var(--dim); font-size:14px; margin:0 0 16px; }
  .bundle { border:1px solid var(--line); border-radius:12px; padding:6px 18px 14px; margin:18px 0; }
  .bundle > h2 { font-size:14px; letter-spacing:.06em; color:var(--dim); margin:10px 0 4px; }
  .bundle > h2 code { color:var(--rec); }
  .problem { font-size:14px; margin:0 0 8px; }
  .q { background:var(--panel); border:1px solid var(--line); border-radius:10px; padding:14px 18px; margin:12px 0; }
  .q h3 { margin:0 0 6px; font-size:15px; }
  .q h3 span { color:var(--acc); margin-right:8px; font-family:ui-monospace, monospace; }
  .why { color:var(--dim); font-size:13.5px; margin:0 0 12px; }
  code { background:#0b0d11; padding:1px 5px; border-radius:4px; font-size:12.5px; }
  label.opt { display:block; padding:6px 8px; border-radius:6px; cursor:pointer; }
  label.opt:hover { background:#1e2330; }
  label.opt input { margin-right:8px; }
  .rec { color:var(--rec); font-size:12px; font-weight:600; margin-left:8px; }
  textarea { width:100%; box-sizing:border-box; background:#0b0d11; color:var(--fg); border:1px solid var(--line); border-radius:6px; padding:8px; min-height:44px; font:inherit; margin-top:8px; }
  #out { white-space:pre-wrap; background:#0b0d11; border:1px solid var(--line); border-radius:8px; padding:12px; font:12.5px ui-monospace, monospace; display:none; }
  .toast { position:fixed; bottom:20px; right:20px; background:var(--rec); color:#000; padding:8px 14px; border-radius:6px; display:none; font-weight:600; }
</style>
</head>
<body>
<header>
  <h1>${esc(title)}</h1>
  <button class="ghost" onclick="preview()">Preview</button>
  <button onclick="copyAll()">Copy answers</button>
</header>
<main>
<p class="lead">One bundle per problem, a marker, at most five questions, options + comment, a bundle comment. Pick an option or write your own in the comment. Copy answers and paste the block back into chat.</p>
${body}
<div id="out"></div>
</main>
<div class="toast" id="toast">Copied</div>
<script>
document.querySelectorAll('input[type=radio]').forEach(r=>{ r.addEventListener('mousedown',function(){ this._was=this.checked; }); r.addEventListener('click',function(){ if(this._was){ this.checked=false; } }); });
function collect(){
  const lines=[];
  document.querySelectorAll(".bundle").forEach(b=>{
    lines.push(\`## \${b.dataset.marker}\`);
    b.querySelectorAll(".q").forEach(q=>{
      const id=q.dataset.id;
      const picked=[...q.querySelectorAll("input:checked")].map(i=>i.value);
      const c=q.querySelector("textarea")?.value.trim();
      lines.push(\`### \${id}\`); lines.push(\`answer: \${picked.length?picked.join(" | "):"(unanswered)"}\`); if(c) lines.push(\`comment: \${c}\`);
    });
    const bc=b.querySelector(\`textarea[name="\${b.dataset.marker}_bc"]\`)?.value.trim();
    if(bc) lines.push(\`bundle comment: \${bc}\`);
    lines.push("");
  });
  return lines.join("\\n");
}
function preview(){ const o=document.getElementById("out"); o.textContent=collect(); o.style.display="block"; o.scrollIntoView({behavior:"smooth"}); }
async function copyAll(){ const t=collect(); try{ await navigator.clipboard.writeText(t); }catch(e){ preview(); return; } const s=document.getElementById("toast"); s.style.display="block"; setTimeout(()=>s.style.display="none",1500); }
</script>
</body>
</html>`;
fs.writeFileSync(out, html);
console.log(`rendered ${bundles.length} open bundle(s) -> ${out}`);
EOF
cat > "$SP/ingest-answers.mjs" <<'EOF'
// Usage: node ingest-answers.mjs <interview-dir> <answers.txt>
// Parses "## <marker> / ### <id> / answer: / comment: / bundle comment:" blocks and writes them into the bundle files.
import fs from 'node:fs'; import path from 'node:path';
const [dir, file] = process.argv.slice(2);
const text = fs.readFileSync(file,'utf8');
let marker=null, qid=null; const got={};
for (const raw of text.split('\n')) {
  const line = raw.trimEnd();
  let m;
  if ((m=line.match(/^## (\S+)/))) { marker=m[1]; qid=null; got[marker]={questions:{}}; continue; }
  if (!marker) continue;
  if ((m=line.match(/^### (\S+)/))) { qid=m[1]; got[marker].questions[qid]={}; continue; }
  if ((m=line.match(/^bundle comment:\s?(.*)$/))) { got[marker].bundle_comment=m[1]; qid=null; continue; }
  if (qid && (m=line.match(/^answer:\s?(.*)$/))) { got[marker].questions[qid].answer=m[1]; continue; }
  if (qid && (m=line.match(/^comment:\s?(.*)$/))) { got[marker].questions[qid].comment=m[1]; continue; }
  if (qid && line && got[marker].questions[qid].comment!==undefined) { got[marker].questions[qid].comment += '\n'+line; continue; }
  if (line && got[marker].bundle_comment!==undefined && !qid) { got[marker].bundle_comment += '\n'+line; }
}
for (const [marker, data] of Object.entries(got)) {
  const p = path.join(dir,'bundles',`${marker}.json`);
  if (!fs.existsSync(p)) { console.error(`no bundle file for ${marker}`); continue; }
  const b = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(p,'utf8'));
  let all=true;
  for (const q of b.questions) {
    const a = data.questions[q.id];
    if (a) { q.answer = a.answer ?? '(unanswered)'; if (a.comment) q.comment = a.comment; }
    if (!q.answer || q.answer==='(unanswered)') { if (!(q.comment)) all=false; }
  }
  if (data.bundle_comment) b.bundle_comment = data.bundle_comment;
  b.state = all ? 'answered' : 'open';
  fs.writeFileSync(p, JSON.stringify(b,null,2)+'\n');
  console.log(`${marker}: ${b.state}`);
}
EOF

const fs=require('fs'); const p=process.argv[2]; const s=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(p,'utf8'));
const prev={ v:s.version, file:'spec.v1.json', date:'2026-09-03', commit:s.commit, reason:s.reason };
s.previous_versions=[...(s.previous_versions||[]), prev];
s.version=2; s.status='draft'; delete s.commit;
s.name='flightcrew-core';
s.reason='Second attempt at the flightcrew system: the run it drove was accepted with reservations and its record shows the command surface shaping every decision; v2 keeps the intention, states what v1 left tacit, and replaces command-driven behaviours with core system behaviours over one schema-governed document language.';
const v1Intent=s.intent.text;
s.intent={ id:'INT', status:'changed',
  text:"A single developer hands a frozen spec to an orchestrated run and leaves the room. The developer's knowledge is front-loaded before the build, never asked for during it; the work is decomposed into units that isolated agents attempt until each is deterministically done; results are verifiable; the developer meets the run only at fixed checkpoints and hard stops; and every artefact the run reads or writes is a document in one schema-governed language, so the system is improved by iteration rather than rebuilt. The system is agent-shaped. It exists so that one AI engineer can carry work of larger scope and complexity than one-shot chat turns allow.",
  note:'v1 stated the outcome (leave the room; plan, build in isolated units, prove with locked checks, fresh critic, provenance, three endings) and left tacit what made it possible. v2 adds knowledge front-loaded before the build, checkpoints and hard stops as the only human contact, and one document language as the foundation, and drops the enumeration of mechanisms that belong in behaviours.' };
// v1 nodes: all already ok; ensure reset
for (const k of ['scope','constraints','interfaces','behaviours','edges','decisions']) for (const n of s[k]) { if (n.status!=='ok') { n.status='ok'; delete n.note; } }
for (const k of ['verification','acceptance']) { if (s[k].status!=='ok') { s[k].status='ok'; delete s[k].note; } }
s.retired = s.retired || [];
fs.writeFileSync(p, JSON.stringify(s,null,2)+'\n');
console.log('v1 intent was:', v1Intent);
EOF

[ASSISTANT 💬]
Right — attack the intent, not decorate it. Here is what I find when I try to break it, using the record of the first attempt as evidence.

**Does it hold?**

- **"Front-loads everything they know"** is unfalsifiable and, in the strong sense, false: run 1 froze a spec and still hit two checks no faithful implementation could pass; run 2 carried B39, B27, I14 and C3 conflicts into its reservations. Every run so far found something the spec did not hold. The claim that survives is weaker and sharper: *no decision is made during the build that the engineer did not make before it* — where the spec is silent, the run stops and the spec changes. That is a rule the system can enforce; "everything" is a hope.
- **"Leaves the room"** contradicts "stops at checkpoints" unless presence is bounded, not absent. Run 2 took 243 minutes with you present and the log recorded its cost as "not recorded" — the record could not even say how much of you it spent. As written the claim can never be checked. It holds only if the run counts every human intervention (gate, escalation, reservation) and the intent claims a bounded number: three gates and one diagnosis.
- **"Proves its result"** was not achieved in v1: the run was accepted *with reservations*, meaning the proof missed what you found by hand. The source says the only defence against a false green is an end-to-end proof that exercises the result the way its user would. This system's user is an agent running a launch. So either the acceptance of flightcrew-core is a launch conducted by flightcrew-core, or "proves" stays flaky. That is the hardest consequence of your intent and you have not been asked it.
- **"Each run's record improves the next"** is currently faked. The log's human fields (cause, change, watch) were written by the orchestrator — `cause: draft (orchestrator)` is in the file. The improvement loop is the human diagnosis feeding the next setup, and nothing stops a next run starting before the diagnosis exists. The claim holds only with an enforcement twin: no run of a launch starts while the previous run's diagnosis is unwritten.
- **"Outgrown one-shot chat turns"** hides a threshold. The interview costs hours; for feature-sized work that is more than the one-shot cost. The intent is honest only if it says the system is for work *too large* for one-shot turns, not a replacement for them.
- **"Deterministically done"** (your phrase) is true of checks, never of agents. The system is deterministic at its gates and probabilistic between them; the intent should claim proven outcomes, not deterministic agents.

**Will it produce an outcome at all?** It already did once — this repository was built by run 2 — so the outcome part is not flaky. What was flaky is the *absence* part and the *proof* part, and both are flaky for the same reason: nothing in v1 measured or enforced them.

**The intent that survives the attack:**

> An AI engineer takes on work too large for one-shot turns by deciding everything before the build, in a frozen spec, and letting a crew of agents carry it to a proven result: no decision is made during the build that the engineer did not make before it, the run stops where the spec is silent, and the engineer meets it only at three gates and one diagnosis. Every artefact is a document in one schema-governed language, so what a run cost, where it stopped and why is the material the next run is built from.

The attack exposes four forks that only you can rule on; they are the bundle.

[ASSISTANT 💬]
Then here is your intention, as you wrote it, taken apart phrase by phrase against the evidence of what v1 actually did. Each cut ends in the question you and only you can answer; those questions are the bundle.

**"An agent orchestrated workflow"** — not an outcome, a noun for machinery. v1 got a means-shaped intent ("go for it" plus a description of mechanisms) and built a means-shaped system: a runner. The command surface you now resent is the direct product of the intent you handed it. You are about to do it again — this sentence names no work, no result, nothing you could hold at the end. *What is the first piece of non-flightcrew work you will hand this system?* If you cannot name one, the honest intent is "a system whose purpose is to build itself until it can build something else", and then dogfood is the only acceptance test there is.

**"front loads the users knowledge before the build and not during"** — three faults. First, *whose* knowledge: you told me untangling sources is not your role and you think in behaviours and edges. So the knowledge you hold is judgement — what you want, what you'd refuse, which cost you'd pay — and everything else is facts an explorer can fetch. v1's spec failed on the *agent's* knowledge (it described the runner), not yours. Second, "not during" while also asking for "user checkpoints": a checkpoint is knowledge delivered during. Either you define what a gate decides (shape and cost) versus what is front-loaded (behaviour), or the phrase is a contradiction. Third, the price: this interview is already hours in, and "not during" means paying it for every launch. *How many hours of interview per launch will you pay before you would rather be asked during the build?* And the bigger one this session is exposing: you said you cannot write an 80-behaviour spec. Spec-making has no owner in the system (structure P3). *Is the real intent that the spec stage becomes part of the system — that v3's interview is conducted by the thing v2 builds?* That is a much larger claim than what you wrote, and it changes what is core.

**"Outcomes improved by iteration"** — iteration of what, measured by what? v1's log says `cost: not recorded` and its human fields were written by the orchestrator. Nothing was measured between run 1 and run 2, so there was no iteration; there was a repeat with a patched map. Without a number this is a slogan. *Which number tells you run 3 beat run 2* — human interventions, spec versions per accepted launch, cost per landed behaviour, reservations at acceptance? Pick, or the log cannot be designed.

**"isolated agents attempting work until deterministically done"** — "until" is unbounded, and unbounded is exactly your stated fear: "blow a shitton of tokens on nothing". Run 1 had two checks no faithful implementation could pass; an honest "until" loops forever. Deterministic done needs a deterministic *not done*: a cap, and then either you are woken (presence) or the run dies (waste). *Which, and at what N?* And "deterministically" is only true of exit codes. You chose agent-shaped with `[judged]` checks — a judged unit is done when a judge says so. *Do you accept units whose done is an opinion, and who watches the judge?*

**"Verifiable results"** — v1's results *were* verified: every check passed, the run was accepted, and you then found 58 problems by hand. Verification proved the spec and the spec was wrong. The 58 problems were found by judging against the *source*, which no role in v1 held — the critic judges against the spec alone. *Does a role in v2 judge the result against the source pattern as well as the spec?* It costs critic context and inherits the source's contradictions; declining means the next 58 are also found by you.

**"user checkpoints, decomposed work, hard stops and restrictions"** — the source's list, which v1 already built. Naming it again says nothing about what failed, and the record does: gates fail open (system P5), gate three is read by nothing (endings P2), the lock guard misses run-local checks (testing P5), Bash is unguarded (system P4). What failed was not the list but the *enforcement* — every one was an instruction with no twin. *Do you commit to "a restriction that is not enforced by the harness does not exist", including Bash writes, knowing the cost is a PreToolUse guard on every shell command?*

**"All prebuilt into an integrated system"** — v1 was integrated. Its integration was the runner, and integration is what you are unpicking. Integration by shared language instead of shared program means: schemas, validators that run on every write, hooks that fire at every moment that matters — and agents writing JSON directly, which makes agents' JSON reliability the new weak point. I will decide the write path (direct writes, validated by a hook that refuses an invalid document) and record it; strike it out if you disagree.

**"a single document 'language' that encourages later improvement"** — "encourages" is a weasel. v1 had nine schemas and still went wrong, because the schemas described the runner's state, not the work's. A language earns its place by what it makes *impossible*: a run without a frozen spec pointer, a document without provenance, a run that ends unrecorded, a second run before the first is diagnosed. I will write those as behaviours; the intent should not say "encourages".

**"The user having advanced as an ai engineer, needs this system…"** — the one true sentence in the paragraph, and it belongs first, because it is the *why*. Everything above it is *what*.

## Answers

**(1) Script purposes and export styles:**

**flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/:**
- **fc-explore.js** `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/fc-explore.js:1-2`: "fans one explorer agent per open question, each read-only and bounded to its own scope paths" → `export default result` `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/fc-explore.js (tail)`
- **fc-implement.js** `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/fc-implement.js:1-2`: "dispatches one launch's implementer agents wave by wave…validating every return and stopping on the first halt" → `export default result` `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/fc-implement.js (tail)`
- **fc-review.js** `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/fc-review.js:1-2`: "runs the review loop of one launch…fresh critic pass, routable findings, re-verification, fresh pass up to critic_passes ceiling" → `export default result` `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/fc-review.js (tail)`

**.claude/workflows/:**
- **flightcrew-build.js** `.claude/workflows/flightcrew-build.js (head)`: "Build the flightcrew v1 system against its frozen spec…contracts first, seven implementation units in parallel…then integration" → `export const meta` with named export pattern `.claude/workflows/flightcrew-build.js (tail): return {...}`
- **flightcrew-targets.js** `.claude/workflows/flightcrew-targets.js (head)`: "Stage 2 of the flightcrew build: fixtures, suite library, 29 locked testbench suites, draft tests map" → `export const meta` with named export pattern `.claude/workflows/flightcrew-targets.js (tail): return {...}`

**(2) Execution vs parsing:**

The workflows suite **only parses and inspects the scripts; it never executes them**. `flightdeck/testbench/suites/workflows/run.mjs:3`: "Scripts are read, never executed." The test runs `node --experimental-default-type=module --check` with the `--check` flag, which validates syntax without execution `flightdeck/testbench/suites/workflows/run.mjs:10-15`.

**(3) Invocation per manual and README:**

Scripts are invoked as `/fc-explore`, `/fc-implement`, `/fc-review` from Claude Code with structured args objects `flightdeck/manuals/harness/workflows.md:20-30` (table). The `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/README.md:1-10`: "runner is `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc`…Claude Code then runs each as `/&lt;name&gt;`, invoked with its `args` object as structured data — `/fc-explore` with `{ "questions": [ … ] }`, and the others with the objects below."

**(4) Distinct or copied:**

**Distinct and not copies.** The .claude/workflows/ scripts (`flightcrew-build.js` and `flightcrew-targets.js`) serve a different purpose than the three flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/ scripts. They orchestrate the build process itself rather than implement the explorer/implementer/reviewer roles. They do not duplicate any scripts in flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/.

> Having outgrown one-shot chat turns, an AI engineer needs to take on larger and more complex work than a conversation can carry. Flightcrew is a crew of agents that builds what a spec says — beginning with itself, until it can build other things. It flips the approach: the engineer's ideas and vision are front-loaded into a spec with no gaps, however long that takes, and never dispensed through the build. It flips the design: the system introspects its own runs to improve by iteration instead of aiming for a perfect single shot. And it embodies agent best-practice conventions, seeded from the source pattern and superseded by its own record as that record grows.

Now the drill.

const fs=require('fs'); const p=process.argv[2]; const s=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(p,'utf8'));
const byId=(arr,id)=>arr.find(n=>n.id===id);
const change=(arr,id,text,note)=>{ const n=byId(arr,id); n.text=text; n.status='changed'; n.note=note; };
const retire=(arr,id,note)=>{ const i=arr.findIndex(n=>n.id===id); const n=arr[i]; s.retired.push({id:n.id, at:2, text:n.text, note}); arr.splice(i,1); };
const add=(arr,node)=>{ arr.push({...node, status:'new'}); };

s.intent.text="Having outgrown one-shot chat turns, an AI engineer needs to take on larger and more complex work than a conversation can carry. Flightcrew is a crew of agents that builds what a spec says — beginning with itself, until it can build other things. It flips the approach: the engineer's ideas and vision are front-loaded into a spec with no gaps, however long that takes, and never dispensed through the build; judging an outcome leads back to the spec, not into the run. It flips the design: the outcome is discovered across attempts from a definition of done — work decomposed so that cheap agents retry it rather than expensive agents single-shotting it — and the system introspects its own runs to improve by iteration. Its state is shaken out progressively as open documents any program can read, and it embodies agent best-practice conventions, seeded from the source pattern and superseded by its own record as that record grows.";
s.intent.note="v1 stated the outcome as a run the developer could leave and enumerated mechanisms. v2 states why the system exists, what it is (a crew that builds from a spec, itself first) and the flips that distinguish it from a human-in-the-loop build: vision front-loaded with no gaps and judged back into the spec; outcome discovered across cheap retried attempts; state shaken out progressively as open documents. Mechanisms move to behaviours.";

const chB=(id,text,note)=>{ const n=B(id); n.text=text; n.status='changed'; n.note=note; };
const chE=(id,text,note)=>{ const n=E(id); n.text=text; n.status='changed'; n.note=note; };
const retire=(arr,id,note)=>{ const i=arr.findIndex(n=>n.id===id); const n=arr[i]; s.retired.push({id:n.id, at:2, text:n.text, note}); arr.splice(i,1); };
const addB=(id,text,note)=>s.behaviours.push({id,status:'new',text,note});
const addE=(id,text,note)=>s.edges.push({id,status:'new',text,note});
const D='[deterministic] ', P='[property] ', J='[judged] ';
const okTag=(id)=>{ const n=B(id); if(!n.text.startsWith('[')) { n.text=D+n.text; n.status='changed'; n.note=(n.note?n.note+' ':'')+'Check-class tag added (SC12).'; } };

// ---------- edges: v1 ----------
